================================================================================
PART 66 — v8.2 ADDITIONS: ARCHITECTURAL FEATURE BRIDGE
(JSON/JS blueprint → opt-in field mapping for the 10 RDJ features)
================================================================================
Source: RDJ_PUBLISHERS_LOW_POLY_3D_SYSTEM_COMPREHENSIVE_ARCHITECTURAL_
IMPROVEMENTS.txt. PART 33 documents the 10 features for the TS factory
path. For the JSON/JS blueprint path, the AI does not emit raw Three.js
code, so 8 of the 10 features (AO [1], IK [2], canvas decals [3],
audio FSM [5], particle VFX [6], spring dynamics [8], multi-format
export [9], inverted-hull outline [4 partial]) are renderer-side
behaviors that the renderer already implements for blueprints matching
the right shape — the AI's only job on the JSON/JS path is to author
blueprints that the renderer can recognize as wanting each feature.

The 4 features where the JSON/JS path has explicit blueprint-side
hooks (features 4 [shading mode], 5 [animation FSM states], 7 [socket
attachment], 10 [standard bone naming]) are documented below. The
remaining 6 are addressed by the existing renderer behavior — the AI
emits the same blueprint it would today and the renderer handles the
rest. See 66.3 for the cross-reference table.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66.1 — BLUEPRINT-SIDE FEATURE HOOKS (the 4 that apply)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

[4] MULTI-STYLE SHADING (blueprint-side hook)
    The renderer recognizes a top-level blueprint field
    `shadingMode: 'flat-pbr' | 'cel-toon' | 'hologram' | 'retro-psx'`
    and switches the rendering pipeline accordingly. Defaults to
    'flat-pbr' when omitted.

    ❌ // wrong casing or unsupported value
       { "shadingmode": "CelToon" }                 // ❌ lowercase key
       { "shadingMode": "wireframe" }               // ❌ not in enum

    ✅ { "shadingMode": "cel-toon" }                // exact kebab-case
       { "shadingMode": "flat-pbr" }                // default
       // or, when not declared at all, the renderer uses 'flat-pbr'

    RULE: `shadingMode` is a top-level (root) blueprint field
    only. Allowed values are exactly the four strings above. Any
    unknown value falls back to 'flat-pbr' and the inspector shows
    a non-blocking warning. Reinforces PART 3 (primitive + material
    catalog) and PART 28 (advanced shading).

[5] ANIMATION FSM STATES (blueprint-side hook)
    The renderer reads an optional `animationFsm: { states: [...] }`
    object on the root group and surfaces each state name as a
    clickable button in the inspector's "Animation" panel. Clips are
    referenced by id (not embedded) — the blueprint's `animations`
    array (existing field) holds the actual THREE.AnimationClip
    objects; the FSM maps human-readable state names to clip ids.

    ❌ // wrong shape (array of strings instead of objects)
       { "animationFsm": ["Idle", "Walk", "Run"] }
       // or: clip embedded in the FSM object instead of by id
       { "animationFsm": {
           "states": [{ "name": "Idle",
                        "clip": { /* inline AnimationClip */ } }]
         } }

    ✅ { "animations": [
           { "id": "clip_idle",  "duration": 2.0 },
           { "id": "clip_walk",  "duration": 1.2 },
           { "id": "clip_run",   "duration": 0.8 },
           { "id": "clip_jump",  "duration": 0.6 }
         ],
         "animationFsm": {
           "states": [
             { "name": "Idle",  "clipId": "clip_idle",  "fadeIn": 0.25 },
             { "name": "Walk",  "clipId": "clip_walk",  "fadeIn": 0.20 },
             { "name": "Run",   "clipId": "clip_run",   "fadeIn": 0.15 },
             { "name": "Jump",  "clipId": "clip_jump",  "fadeIn": 0.10 }
           ]
         } }

    RULE: `animationFsm.states` is an array of {name, clipId, fadeIn?}
    objects. Every `clipId` MUST match an existing `animations[].id`
    in the same blueprint. At minimum, supply Idle + Walk + Run +
    Jump (matches the master prompt line 6 in PART 33.2). The
    renderer instantiates the FSM lazily — when no FSM is declared,
    the inspector shows the raw clip list instead.

[7] MODULAR SOCKET ATTACHMENT (blueprint-side hook)
    The renderer recognizes a `sockets` object on the root group
    AND on individual parts (via `userData.sockets`) and exposes
    each socket in the inspector's "Kitbash" panel. The AI's job
    is to declare sockets with the canonical names so accessories
    snap to the right place.

    ❌ // wrong socket name (no exact match)
       { "sockets": {
           "head_top":    { "id": "..." },   // ❌ not canonical
           "arm-left":    { "id": "..." },   // ❌ case mismatch
           "weapon-hand": { "id": "..." }    // ❌ not in catalog
         } }

    ✅ { "sockets": {
           "Socket_HeadTop":   { "attachTo": "head",    "offset": [0, 0.18, 0] },
           "Socket_Arm_Left":  { "attachTo": "arm_l",   "offset": [0,  0,    0.15] },
           "Socket_Arm_Right": { "attachTo": "arm_r",   "offset": [0,  0,    0.15] },
           "Socket_Backpack":  { "attachTo": "chest",   "offset": [0, -0.05, -0.18] }
         } }

    SOCKET NAME CATALOG (the renderer uses these EXACT strings to
    index its kitbash library — never rename, never lowercase):

       Socket_HeadTop       (head top — beacons, antennae, hats)
       Socket_Arm_Left      (left hand — cannons, shields, tools)
       Socket_Arm_Right     (right hand — cannons, shields, tools)
       Socket_Backpack      (back — jetpacks, radar, battery packs)

    Per-socket `attachTo` is a string id of an existing part id in
    the same blueprint; `offset` is a local [x, y, z] triple.
    Reinforces PART 30.7 (TS-side mirror).

[10] UNIVERSAL RIGGING & BONE HIERARCHY STANDARD (blueprint-side hook)
    The renderer matches the TS-path STANDARD_BONES catalog
    (PART 33.1[10]) on the JSON/JS side via the `actionProfile.
    animationRole` field (existing field, see PART 30 / 64) AND via
    standard part id strings. The AI's job is to name every joint-
    relevant part with the canonical id so animations and accessories
    are cross-compatible.

    ❌ // non-canonical part ids
       { "id": "leftThigh",  "type": "capsule" }   // ❌ camelCase
       { "id": "right-calf", "type": "capsule" }   // ❌ kebab-case
       { "id": "BoneFootL",  "type": "box" }       // ❌ mixed

    ✅ { "id": "Bone_Thigh_L", "type": "capsule",
         "userData": {
           "actionProfile": { "animationRole": "limb" }
         } }
       { "id": "Bone_Calf_L",  "type": "capsule",
         "userData": {
           "actionProfile": { "animationRole": "limb" }
         } }
       { "id": "Bone_Foot_L",  "type": "box",
         "userData": {
           "actionProfile": { "animationRole": "limb" }
         } }
       // ... mirror for Bone_Thigh_R / Bone_Calf_R / Bone_Foot_R ...

    CANONICAL BONE / JOINT ID CATALOG (the renderer expects these
    EXACTLY when the model is a humanoid / biped / mech):

       Bone_Root       Bone_Pelvis       Bone_Spine       Bone_Chest
       Bone_Head       Bone_Thigh_L      Bone_Calf_L      Bone_Foot_L
       Bone_Toe_L      Bone_Thigh_R      Bone_Calf_R      Bone_Foot_R
       Bone_Toe_R

    RULE: every joint-relevant part id is one of the strings above,
    exactly (case + underscores matter). The renderer's animation
    library uses these ids to map shared clips to the right bones
    across all humanoid / biped / mech blueprints. Reinforces
    PART 30.2 (TS-side) and PART 64 (character enrichment).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66.2 — RENDERER-SIDE FEATURES (the 6 the AI doesn't have to author)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The remaining 6 features are pure renderer behavior. The AI emits
the same blueprint it does today and the renderer implements the
feature when it recognizes the right shape:

  [1] Procedural vertex AO    → renderer applies per-vertex cavity
                                shading automatically when
                                `material.vertexColors` is true
                                (the renderer sets it on by default
                                for PBR materials). AI just uses
                                PBR materials as PART 7 says.
  [2] Analytical 2-bone IK    → renderer exposes a global
                                `ikSolver` on the inspector that
                                drives any 2-segment limb pair
                                automatically. No AI authoring needed.
  [3] Canvas decal synthesizer → renderer's existing PART 8 procedural
                                texture system handles every pattern
                                the AI could want; AI just references
                                the existing `texturePattern` field
                                (or the companion `textureImage`
                                field per PART 65 error [5]).
  [6] Procedural particle VFX → renderer auto-emits exhaust / spark /
                                steam particles for any part marked
                                `userData.isEmitter: true`. AI just
                                declares the field and the renderer
                                handles the rest.
  [8] Spring dynamics         → renderer applies spring-damper
                                physics to any bone whose userData
                                declares `spring: { stiffness, damping,
                                axis }`. AI just provides the values.
  [9] Multi-format export     → renderer's inspector offers GLB / OBJ
                                / STL / USDZ export buttons for every
                                loaded model. AI doesn't author export
                                code on the JSON/JS path.

If the AI declares any of the above via `userData` (e.g. `isEmitter:
true`, `spring: { ... }`), the renderer picks it up automatically.
If the AI doesn't declare them, the model simply doesn't have that
feature — no penalty, no broken-state failure.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66.3 — WHY ONLY 4 OF 10 FEATURES APPLY HERE (cross-reference table)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The full architectural catalog has 10 features. For the JSON/JS
blueprint path, the AI authors structured data, not Three.js code,
so 6 of the 10 features are pure renderer behavior (66.2) and 4 have
explicit blueprint-side hooks (66.1):

  [1] procedural vertex AO       → renderer-side (66.2)
  [2] analytical 2-bone IK       → renderer-side (66.2)
  [3] canvas decal synthesizer   → renderer-side (66.2)
  [4] multi-style shading        → BLUEPRINT FIELD  (66.1)
  [5] audio-reactive FSM         → BLUEPRINT FIELD  (66.1)
  [6] procedural particle VFX    → renderer-side (66.2)
  [7] modular socket attachment  → BLUEPRINT FIELD  (66.1)
  [8] secondary spring dynamics  → renderer-side (66.2)
  [9] multi-format export        → renderer-side (66.2)
  [10] universal rigging standard → BLUEPRINT FIELD  (66.1)

The TS-path full feature catalog is in PART 33 (Prompt_To_Ts.txt /
Image_To_Ts.txt). The JSON/JS-path feature bridge is in this PART 66.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66.4 — STRUCTURAL REFERENCE CHECKLIST (JSON/JS pre-emission additions)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Append these to PART 65's 10-item checklist. Any unchecked item is
either a missing feature hook or a feature hook with a wrong shape.

  [ ] 11. [PART 66.1] If the model needs a non-default shading style,
         `shadingMode` is declared at the root with one of the four
         exact kebab-case enum values.
  [ ] 12. [PART 66.1] If the model has multiple animations, an
         `animationFsm.states` array is declared with at least the
         Idle + Walk + Run + Jump states, every `clipId` matches a
         real `animations[].id`.
  [ ] 13. [PART 66.1] If the model needs kitbash sockets, a `sockets`
         object is declared with the EXACT canonical socket names
         (Socket_HeadTop, Socket_Arm_Left, Socket_Arm_Right,
         Socket_Backpack) and every `attachTo` matches a real part
         id in the same blueprint.
  [ ] 14. [PART 66.1] Every joint-relevant part id is one of the
         canonical bone strings (Bone_Root, Bone_Pelvis, Bone_Spine,
         Bone_Chest, Bone_Head, Bone_Thigh_L/R, Bone_Calf_L/R,
         Bone_Foot_L/R, Bone_Toe_L/R) — exact case + underscores.
  [ ] 15. [PART 66.2] If the model needs procedural particles, every
         emitter part declares `userData.isEmitter: true` and the
         expected `userData.particlePattern` field; the renderer
         auto-creates the THREE.Points / InstancedMesh on load.
  [ ] 16. [PART 66.2] If the model needs spring dynamics on any
         bone, the part's `userData.spring` is an object with
         non-zero `stiffness` AND `damping` AND a valid `axis`
         string ('x' | 'y' | 'z').
  [ ] 17. [PART 65.5] Every socket and bone id mentioned in
         `sockets[].attachTo` and `animationFsm.states[].clipId` is
         actually present in the blueprint (no dangling references).
  [ ] 18. [PART 65.5] Every `sockets` and `animationFsm` declaration
         sits at the ROOT of the blueprint (or inside a part's
         `userData`), never as a sibling of the part list at the
         wrong depth.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
66.5 — INTERSECTION WITH EXISTING PARTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
This PART does NOT replace any existing rule — it adds OPT-IN feature
hooks on top of the JSON/JS bug-fix foundation laid by PART 65 and
the character / mascot / creature enrichment laid by PART 64. The 4
blueprint-side hooks (66.1) and the 4 renderer-side behaviors
(66.2) are independent of every other PART — they layer cleanly on
top, the renderer never throws on missing fields, and the AI is free
to omit any feature a model doesn't need.

Cross-reference map:
  [4] shadingMode         → PART 3 (presets), PART 28 (advanced)
  [5] animationFsm        → PART 5 (animation fields), PART 30
  [7] sockets             → PART 30.7, PART 64 (character enrichment)
  [10] STANDARD_BONES ids → PART 30.2, PART 64 (actionProfile)

PART 66 is the JSON/JS-path feature bridge. If the AI remembers ONLY
one thing from this PART, remember the four canonical name catalogs
in 66.1: `shadingMode` enum, `animationFsm.states[]` shape, the four
canonical `Socket_*` names, and the twelve canonical `Bone_*` ids.

================================================================================
END OF PART 66 — ARCHITECTURAL FEATURE BRIDGE (JSON/JS PATH)
Output remains a valid LBL JSON/JS blueprint. The 4 blueprint-side
hooks in 66.1, the 6 renderer-side behaviors in 66.2, the cross-
reference table in 66.3, the 8-item checklist addition in 66.4, and
the cross-reference map in 66.5 are all OPT-IN — drop them in if and
only if the model actually exercises the corresponding feature. The
structural checklist in 66.4 is MANDATORY only for the features a
model has enabled. Nothing in this PART removes or weakens any rule
from PARTs 1-65.
